## Current source restrictions

# Frozen source and assessment boundaries

This assessment covers Matt Dillahunty and Randal Rauser’s opening speeches and direct debate, including the concluding exchange, approximately 0:22–1:09:31. Moderator logistics and promotional breaks are excluded. There are no paired formal closing speeches. Audience Q&A, its replies, the brief post-Q&A exchange and the host outro are excluded. Neither speaker receives credit, penalties or an inference of failing to reply from excluded material or the moderator’s final cutoff. The question concerns which framework is better for society; these scores do not determine whether Christianity is true.

1. Assess only Matt Dillahunty and Randal Rauser in their opening speeches and direct debate, including the concluding exchange, approximately 0:22–1:09:31. Exclude every enumerated host/logistics interval and all audience Q&A, dependent answers, post-Q&A exchange, thanks and promotional material. No credit, penalty, reply link or absence inference may come from excluded material. No paired formal closing speeches occur.

2. The motion compares which framework is better for society and promotes human flourishing in this life. Both speakers set aside proving whether Christianity or humanism is ultimately true. Do not convert the motion to proving God, Christianity, atheism, or the resurrection. Assess demands for public justification and practical grounding as actually argued; do not declare stipulated premises established facts.

3. Dillahunty grants historical Christian benefits and says humanism can adopt beneficial practices from other sources. His claim is revisability, equal human worth, cooperation, and publicly assessable consequences. He concedes that humanists can violate their own principles. His equal-voting argument distinguishes a categorical sex exclusion from individual restrictions or abolishing voting for everyone; retain this qualification.

4. Rauser defends mere Christianity associated with the Apostles Creed and emulating Jesus; he is egalitarian, acknowledges Christian failures and shared humanist values, and rejects treating American dispensationalism as all Christianity. Preserve his intrinsic image-of-God account, the difference between rights existing and being codified, his point that neither framework has proved its premises, and his fallibilist rather than infallible treatment of moral intuitions.

5. The abandoned infant/anencephaly case concerns worth without self-valuation or others valuing the person. Do not turn Matt’s assisted-dying aside into endorsement of killing an infant, or attribute involuntary euthanasia to either speaker. The chimpanzee/fire case is an intuition/coherence challenge with a consequentialist qualification, not an independently conducted experiment.

6. Automatic captions sometimes corrupt names and terms (Dillahunty, Carl Sagan, Mary Warnock, imago Dei, anencephalic, complementarian, noetic, ratiocination). Do not silently correct an exact quote or penalize recognition noise. Selected materially uncertain words, references, mixed speaker spans or quotations require below-high confidence and a precise audioVerificationReason. Context can support speaker identity but is not audio verification.

7. Rolling caption durations overlap handoffs; choose complete single-speaker evidence spans conservatively. Event 328 (zero-based) mixes the end of Matt’s opening with the moderator; it is excluded. End-of-debate Matt fragment at 1:09:30 is cut off by the moderator and is not a complete rebuttal. No failure-to-reply penalty may arise from that cutoff. Mixed turns around 54:50,57:20,58:50 and 1:08 require particular attribution care.

8. The only caption gap over five seconds is 7.560 seconds near 1:46:33–1:46:40, wholly inside the excluded host outro. There is no material timed gap in the retained debate. No direct listening or independent audio verification has yet occurred.

9. Historical, theological, psychological, legal and scientific assertions are assessed as arguments presented in this recording, not independently verified facts. Do not import outside research, other debate scores, or unseen replies.

10. Featured quotations must be exact complete 3–18 word thoughts with clear referents. Retain necessary challenges or qualifications in the quote or context; avoid incomplete concessions and corrupted recognition.

The full indexed transcript remains available for source coverage, but excluded portions are not scoring or publication evidence. E labels are one-based; source array indices are zero-based. Selected below-high-confidence moves must be audio-verified before adjudication. Current paid cost is $0; any needed paid inputs require a disclosed cumulative estimate first.

All 41 moves are frozen across seven sections. All 20 uncertain-source checks are completed through five automatic transcriptions of matching official publisher audio. No direct listening is claimed. Completed findings supersede historical pending-audio and cost-zero language only. Captions and exclusions remain fixed. Rauser completes his renewed-creation reply; only Matt’s next fragment is cut off. The a-posteriori wording was confirmed by automatic audio comparison and its substantive conceptual use may be assessed; caption recognition errors may not.

Tags describe only material conduct present in the recoverable source and already reflected in the frozen critique. They never alter ratings. Use only the exact local catalog definitions, not nearby informal meanings. Contested premises, incomplete mechanisms, weak analogies and failure to exclude alternatives do not automatically establish circularity, ignorance, equivocation or any other tag. Citation alone is not an appeal to authority. Two tags for one defect require two distinct catalog-defined failures. Zero accepted tags is legitimate; do not invent candidates to meet a quota.

For a blind-review packet, output exactly {schemaVersion:"1.0-source-grounded-rhetorical-review",debateNumber,debateId,reviewedMoveIds,moveReviews,candidateReviews,acceptedTags,audit}. reviewedMoveIds and moveReviews follow the source packet’s final publication order exactly. Each moveReviews item is {moveId,reason}, with at least 22 words identifying the actual inference or qualification and explaining why a plausible definition applies or why the real limitation is not a catalog-defined defect. Write each explanation individually; substitutions of names or roles in generic no-tag prose do not count. candidateReviews includes every serious candidate considered, accepted or rejected, once as {moveId,type,label,decision,rationale}; rationale is at least 20 source-specific words. Accepted rows additionally have context of 8–35 words. acceptedTags is the accepted subset in move order, as {moveId,type,label,url,context,rationale}, with exact catalog URL and identical context/rationale. audit contains exactly completeSourceReviewed, allMovesReviewed, otherReviewerUnavailable, scoresUnavailable, sourceRestrictionsRespected, noScoreChanges, all true only if actually satisfied.

For an anonymous-adjudication packet, read every union candidate and the entire source. Output exactly {schemaVersion:"1.0-source-grounded-rhetorical-adjudication",debateNumber,debateId,decisions,audit}. decisions contains every packet candidate exactly once in its order as {moveId,type,label,decision,rationale}, adding context only for acceptance. Decide each independently from the exact definition, complete source and frozen critique; do not vote-count or add candidates. Rationales are at least 20 words; accepted contexts are 8–35 words. audit contains exactly completeSourceReviewed, allCandidatesReviewed, passIdentitiesUnavailable, scoresUnavailable, sourceRestrictionsRespected, noScoreChanges, all true only if satisfied. The packet determines the complete union length; never substitute the move count.
